**Tests**: Mandatory. This feature's spec and constitution (11.4.224, 11.4.27, 11.4.169) require test-first work with the RED run observed before implementation and every PASS backed by machine-produced evidence. Tasks marked `[TDD]` name the RED condition.

**Organization (deviation from the template, stated)**: the template groups phases by user story. This feature is an audit-and-remediation programme whose work packages cut across all seven user stories, and docs/21 fixes the execution order by dependency (P0 foundation to P7 closure). The phases below therefore follow docs/21 P0-P7; every task carries its `[USn]` tag so a story-ordered view is a filter, not a re-plan. Phase P0 plays the template's Setup role, P1-P2 its Foundational role (no audit or fix work starts before P1 exit and the P2 baselines), and P3-P7 carry the user-story work (US1 register, US2 audit, US3 fixes and evidence, US4 documentation, US5 per-application coverage, US6 dependencies, US7 commit and push hygiene).

## Format: `- [ ] Tnnn [P] [USn] [markers] Description with exact path`

- **[P]**: parallel-safe (different files, no unfinished dependency)
- **[USn]**: user story from spec.md (US1..US7)
- **[TDD]**: RED-GREEN-REFACTOR, RED observed on the broken artifact before implementation
- **[REVIEW]**: independent Opus-xhigh review gate (11.4.142/11.4.209), reviewer stream separate from producer (11.4.240)
- **[SUBAGENT]**: can be delegated to a subagent
- **BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn**: waits for the owner decision (doc21 section 8, research.md OD ids); the safe default is stated where research.md defines one
- Paths marked `(new)` do not exist yet and were chosen by the plan; the reviewer confirms them. In every phase, a path that a task creates and that does not exist at the plan commit is a deliverable of the task that names it, whether or not it carries the mark (the phase-group blocks restate this)

## Constraints that bind every task

Rootless Podman containers only, builds distributed via the containers submodule (11.4.173; until the owner answers ODG-07, images are built and containers run on the local host as a tracked deviation of the P0-P1 conventions, never claimed compliant); no CI/CD (11.4.156); no force-push, ff-only to all upstreams, a diverged branch integrated by a merge commit and never by a rebase or reset (11.4.113, T042); all work on main branches (FR-024); no credential value in any file (11.4.10); a real service or device is required for every non-unit check, and when it is absent the result is `blocked-unavailable`, never a pass (spec clarification A2); every fix leaves a RED, three GREEN, a caught mutation and an independent GO review (docs/04, docs/06).

## Shared abbreviations (apply to every phase)

| Abbreviation | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `$FEAT` = `$SPEC` | `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation` |
| `$EV` | `$FEAT/evidence` (ledger.jsonl of `ev/1` entries, class `evidence-ledger` with its own size bound (check class row, T040b), anchors, blobs, verdicts; docs/06 section 11); fixed subfolders `reviews/` (every `[REVIEW]` verdict), `hc/` (human checkpoints), `coverage_baseline/<app>/` and `coverage_targets/<app>/` (never a folder named `coverage/`, which `.gitignore:139` ignores at any depth); every other `$EV/<name>/` folder or `$EV/<name>.json` file is owned by the task that first writes it, which names it |
| `$CPA_RUN` | the run directory of one CPA run, `.audit/commit-push/<run_id>/` (ignored by the T004 `/.audit/` rule, never tmpfs, so a crashed run leaves its stage files for the anti-mess sweep; named `$EVID` until rev 8, renamed because docs/08 §6.1 uses `$EVID` for `$EV/web`); `<run_id>` is the UTC start time to the second, the process id and a random suffix (for example `20261003T120000Z-41234-9f3ac2e1`), so two runs never share one; every output of the run lives there and nowhere else: `report.json` (written last, holding the sha256 of every other file of the directory), `summary.txt`, the stage transcripts, the S7 verifier JSON `verify.json`, the deferral rows, the `/out` directories of the run's container checks, its disk-headroom records, the §9.2 backup bundles of a merge integration under `backup/`, the merge record `merge.json` written before `git merge` starts, with the run's process id and process start time (so a merge left in progress is attributed to its run, as interrupted only once that process is gone and as the lock holder while it lives, T040, T042) and the conflicting files of a refused merge under `conflicts/` (T042); the resolver of a refused merge writes its resolution under the ignored `.audit/merge-resolution/<run_id>/` of the refused run, which CPA only reads (T042); the only files outside it that CPA writes are the ignored `.audit/pending_pins.tsv` (T042a) and the lock and registry records under the ignored `.audit/longops/`, written through `scripts/longops/acquire.sh` and `release.sh` (T089) |
| CPA run record | what a task cites for a CPA run, since nothing of the run lands in the tracked tree: after the run has exited, the caller captures `$CPA_RUN/report.json` and `$CPA_RUN/verify.json` with `tools/evidence/evrec -- cat <file>` (an `ev/1` entry whose stdout blob `$EV/blobs/<sha256>` is the file byte for byte, so its sha256 is the file's), or, before the recorder exists (T050), copies them into the task's `$EV/<wp>/` folder with their lines in `$EV/<wp>/SHA256SUMS`; the entry, blob or copy is an ordinary member of the caller's next declared change set, committed by a later CPA run; git itself records every run that committed (its `CPA-Run:` trailer) |
| clean tracked tree | what every clean-tree gate measures (the T161 freeze, the `dirty` field of the T225 and T300 run manifests, T085, T178, T302 and the WP-73 final checks): `scripts/repo/verify_repos.sh --no-remote` (T032) reports `summary.dirty` equal to `summary.dirty_excepted`, each repository judged by `git status --porcelain --ignore-submodules=all` with untracked files counted and ignored paths such as `.audit/` outside it, excepted rows coming only from the reviewed `scripts/repo/exceptions.tsv` (T032); pointer drift is reported beside it, a drift equal to a row of `.audit/pending_pins.tsv` named as a pending pin move (T042a); because CPA writes nothing into the tracked tree, the tree is clean right after a CPA run that exits 0 or 14; the gate cites, by its report sha256, the CPA run record of the run that made the measured HEAD (its run id read from the `CPA-Run:` trailer of that commit, so a later run that committed nothing cannot change the citation; this table governs the rule, which docs/02 §12.1 (rev 7), docs/06 §11 rule 4, docs/16 rule 5, docs/12 and docs/21 IC-42 and IC-47 (rev 11) state as well), or records `report_sha256: null` with a reason when HEAD is not a CPA commit or that report is absent on this host; a test asserts this condition, never a raw `git status --porcelain`, which prints ` M <path>` for a submodule whose content is dirty or whose HEAD moved even when the tracked tree is clean (plan owner's rule (f), rev 9) |
| `$AUD` | `$FEAT/audit`; one `finding/1` file per finding at `$AUD/findings/<FND-NNNN>.json` and the derived per-run index `$AUD/runs/<run>/findings.index.jsonl` (P3 entry block); no audit output goes under a folder named `out/` (`.gitignore:112`) |
| `$WI` / `$DB` | the constitution `workable-items` binary and `docs/workable_items.db` (ODG-11 default, OD-11) |
| `$FEAT/contracts/` | the JSON schemas of this feature; every schema path is written with the `$FEAT/` prefix because containers run with `--workdir /src` (the repository root), where a bare `contracts/` path does not exist |
| `locked.sh` | `scripts/register/locked.sh -- <cmd>` (T064): a host control-plane launcher that takes `flock docs/.register.lock` (docs/04 §12.2 single writer) and runs exactly one mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command against `$DB` inside IMG-TESTUTIL through `RUNP --rw docs`, so the image's `sqlite3` and the engine binary write the register and a host `sqlite3` (the only one on `PATH` at plan review was an Android SDK copy) never does; binary versions and sha256 in `$EV/wp06/register-binaries.json`; every mutating register command from T069 on goes through it, and a bare mutating call is a review finding |
| ratchet baseline | `scripts/repo/validate_baselines/<check>.tsv` (T040): one row per `(check, path, rule)` with its measured count, for the repository-wide checks that CPA stage S3 runs, each registered in `scripts/repo/validate_checks.tsv` (the anti-bluff scan and the revision-header check, T040; `check_pins.sh`, T105; the QA bank validator, T210); measured, in the change set that registers the check, over the check's scope in the main repository and in every own-organisation repository at every depth, each path keyed from the main-repository root as the secret baseline is (T040a; plan owner's rule (W), rev 10), so a `--repo` run or a main-mode commit inside a submodule scores that repository against its own rows and never meets its existing violations as new keys (T042a); S3 fails when a count of a declared file rises, when a new key appears in a declared file, or when a declared file's count falls below its row while the change set leaves the row unchanged, so a baseline may only shrink and follows every reduction (§11.4.135); under `--repo` a declared file whose count falls below its row is reported `baseline_lowering_owed` (key, row count, new count, run id), never 10, because a `--repo` change set cannot declare the main-repository baseline file, and the main-mode change set that moves that repository's gitlink (G-PIN: T442, T581) lowers the row: the files a gitlink move declared in a change set brings into the main tree (those that differ between the old and the new recorded commit, nested gitlinks followed) are held to the lowering rule as declared files of that change set, so the move without the lowering gives 10 (plan owner's decision after the round-11 reviews, T040, T042a); the revision-header check applies to every declared Markdown file of a class that applies it, whatever its row, so a file that a change set edits gains the header and its row is lowered in the same change set (T040); a key outside the declared files that an S1 integration brought in (another clone's commits, the gitlinks they move included), and a new or raised key in the files a declared gitlink move brings in from another actor's commits, never this change set's own work, is reported `baseline_drift`, never a refusal of the run that reports it, and registered at the next window as a finding through the T092 path, whose owed action is a fix of the file, never a row added or a count raised: a key above its row or absent from the baseline closes when a change set removes the violation (until then a change set that declares that file gives 10, because there its key is new or above its row), and a count that the drift lowered closes when a change set lowers the row to it (T040). Bootstrap rule: a baseline file is created only in the change set that registers its check, with a header naming the commit it was measured on and the review verdict that covers it, which exists with GO when the file is committed: that verdict holds GO in the main-repository HEAD or in the same declared change set as an unheld path (it then lands in the same commit as the baseline), or the baseline file and its registration are committed as a held commit whose awaited verdict is exactly the verdict the header names, S6 still pushing nothing before that GO (plan owner's rule (X), rev 10); after that, no row is added and no count raised |
| check class (rule (V)) | which S2 and S3 checks apply to a file (plan owner's rule (V), rev 11, with the decisions taken after the round-11 reviews, rev 12; T040b): its class in the reviewed path-class table `scripts/repo/check_exemptions.tsv` (path glob from the main-repository root to class, in git-wildmatch semantics, where `*` stays inside one directory and `**/` matches zero or more directories; a row that names an exact path takes precedence over glob rows, and glob rows of two different classes that match one path are refused, 20 `class_ambiguous`) and that class's row per check in `scripts/repo/check_classes.tsv` (`yes`, `no` or, for the large-file check, a byte bound, each with its reason); classes `source` (every path that no row names: every check, 1,024,000 B), `generated` (`docs/workable_items.db` and `docs/register/**`: no whitespace, end-of-file or revision-header check, 16,777,216 B, the one register bound), `evidence` (anchors, deferrals, blobs, verdicts, run records, manifests, findings, transcripts and the other tool outputs: no whitespace, end-of-file or conflict-marker check), `patches` (`$AUD/patches/*.patch`, byte-exact: no whitespace or end-of-file check), `governance-carrier` (`.specify/memory/constitution-appendix.md` and `submodules/constitution/Constitution.md`: the private-key check skips only the exact lines that `scripts/repo/private_key_carriers.tsv` lists, a list valid in every class, 4,194,304 B), `fixtures` (under a root of `scripts/repo/fixture_roots.txt`: every check less those the root's row names), `evidence-ledger` (`$EV/ledger.jsonl` and `$EV/flake_ledger.jsonl`: only the secret fold, the private-key check and the conflict-marker guard, 33,554,432 B (32 MiB) derived from the projected entry count, raised only through its held reviewed raise step) and `legacy-collection` (`docs/issues/**` and the 41 S-12 root reports: every check of `source` but the revision-header check); the closed check set, the checks given the declared files one by one, is `secret_fold`, `private_key`, `merge_conflict`, `trailing_whitespace`, `end_of_file`, `check_yaml`, `check_json`, `large_file`, `shell_parse`, `revision_header`, `anti_bluff`, `check_pins`, `bank_validator` and the language checks `go_fmt`, `go_vet`, `go_imports`, `no_false_positive_log`, `eslint` and `prettier`, extended only by a reviewed registration that adds the new check's row for every class in the same change set (a check registered with scope `files` in `scripts/repo/validate_checks.tsv` that has no class rows is refused, 20 `check_unknown`, never given a default), while a stage registered with scope `changeset`, one that reads the repository or a spec pair rather than the declared files (`scripts/detect-landmines.sh`, `scripts/repo/check_no_ci.sh` and the later contract-diff, coverage, performance and schema-diff stages), is outside the class table; no class exempts a file from the secret fold; a change to these tables counts only with its review, and while it is held on that review only the paths held on the same verdict are judged with the declared tables, every other path with the HEAD tables (`table_admits_unheld_path`, T040b) |
| deliberate-violation fixture | a test fixture that violates a repository-wide check on purpose (a seeded bluff, an unpinned image, a fake secret, a placeholder bank step): by default materialised by its test in a temporary directory, never committed; otherwise committed only under a root listed in the reviewed data file `scripts/repo/fixture_roots.txt` (T040a; each row a root, the checks of the closed check set it is exempt from and the reason, added in the change that creates the root under the table-change rule of T040b; its files are class `fixtures`, check class row), which those checks read as their only exclusion; the same violation outside a listed root still fires (golden-false fixture of T040a) |
| `RUNP <IMG-ID> -- cmd` | base layer: `scripts/containers/run_pinned.sh <IMG-ID> -- cmd` (WP-09); takes an image id, resolves it to `reference@sha256:<digest>` from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml` and composes one rootless `podman run` with the limits, after the disk-headroom precondition `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh` (T001); `RUNP --rw docs` adds the one writable source bind it accepts, `docs/` at `/src/docs` over the read-only source mount, used only by `locked.sh` (T003, T007); `RUNP --out <dir>` binds `<dir>` at `/out`, the only other writable mount (default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`, T003) |
| `runner(IMG-x)` | middle layer: the WP-12 per-toolchain wrapper for image IMG-x (`scripts/containers/run_go.sh`, `run_node.sh`, `run_docs.sh`, `run_scan.sh`, `run_playwright.sh`, `run_testutil.sh` for IMG-TESTUTIL, T120, and `run_qa.sh` for IMG-QA, T212), built on top of `RUNP` and adding the envelope limits, long-op registration and the anti-mess sweep |
| `TIC <app> <lane> -- cmd` | top layer: the WP-12 lane dispatcher `scripts/test-in-container.sh <app> <lane> -- cmd` (T121); takes an application and a lane (not an image id), looks the row up in `scripts/containers/lanes.tsv` and dispatches through the matching `run_*.sh` wrapper (the `tooling` rows to `run_testutil.sh`, IMG-TESTUTIL; the `docs` rows to `run_docs.sh`, IMG-DOCS; the `qa` rows, added by T212, to `run_qa.sh`), which in turn calls `RUNP`-style `podman run`. The three names are layered, not one mechanism: before WP-12 only `RUNP` exists |
| `CPA` | `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (WP-04, docs/21 IC-16): every commit and push from its recorded adoption (T047, after the T046 G-GATE GO) goes through it, in commit windows (P0-P1 conventions), on `main`, pushing fast-forward only, never `--force`, and integrating a diverged repository only by a merge commit it makes itself, never by a rebase or reset (plan owner's rule (Y), rev 10, T042); the declared change set is the explicit path list `--paths-from FILE`, where a path with a review verdict in its second column is committed as a held commit, pushed only once that verdict, read from the main-repository HEAD whatever repository holds the commit, is GO and names the commit's run (review verdict row; `--awaits-review <verdict>` holds every path of the run; in main mode a held path inside a submodule is refused, T042); `--local-only` commits without pushing, `--repo <path>` commits inside one owned repository (T042a), `--commit-before-integrate` commits the declared change set before it integrates a moved remote, the sanctioned remediation of `ff_blocked_by_local_changes` (T042); CPA never writes into the tracked tree and never commits its own outputs, which stay in `$CPA_RUN`; each commit it creates carries the trailer `CPA-Run: <run_id>` and, in its message body, the run's deferral flags (`Deferred-Gates:` with `SKIP_LONG`, `SWEEP_ABSENT` or `LOCAL_ONLY`, `SKIP_LONG` recorded at S0, so a CPA merge commit carries it too), on a held commit the review it waits for (`Awaits-Review: <verdict path>`, a path from the main-repository root), and the trailer-less commits since adoption that a live remote tip held at S1, brought in from another clone or track (`Foreign-Commit: <sha>`; a trailer-less local commit that no remote holds is refused, never named) (T042) |
| review verdict | the verdict file of a `[REVIEW]` task, `$EV/reviews/<gate-or-wp>[-<item>][-r<n>].json`, in the contract `review-verdict/1` (`$FEAT/contracts/review-verdict.schema.json`; CPA's merge-review files `$EV/reviews/CPA-merge-<run_id>.json`, T040, follow the same contract): `schema` (the constant `review-verdict/1`), `verdict` (`GO` or `NO-GO`), `blocking_findings` (the count of blocking findings, 0 for a GO, as docs/16 §12.2.4 requires), `covers_runs` (one entry per held commit the review covers: repository path, commit sha and `CPA-Run` id, read from the commit and its trailers; empty when it covers none, as for every review before CPA adoption, T047), and the model and effort that performed it (plan owner's rule (e), rev 9); a held commit is released only by a GO verdict committed in the main-repository HEAD whose `covers_runs` holds the commit's `CPA-Run` id, a GO only in a working tree or in a submodule's own tree releasing nothing (rule (a)); a GO verdict file is final: its `covers_runs` is written once, by ST-REV, in the version that first holds GO, listing every held commit that awaits that file at that time, which S5 checks over the unpushed range of every owned repository before it commits such a GO (20, `verdict_covers_incomplete`, naming the missing commit, T042), and a hold on a verdict file that holds GO, in the main-repository HEAD or in the same declared change set, or any change to such a file, is refused (20, `verdict_already_go`, rule (c), T042 S5), so work made after a GO waits on the file of a new review iteration (`-r<n>`) and an earlier GO never covers later work |
| `ST-xx` | work stream of docs/21 section 4.1 |
| `HC-n` | human checkpoint of docs/21 section 3.3; every record is written to `$EV/hc/HC-<n>[-<USn>].json` |
| `OD-nn` (open, ungrouped) | one of the 15 open `research.md` decisions of docs/21 section 8.6 that no ODG group covers; imported as its own `Operator-blocked` item (T070, T071) and named `BLOCKED-ON OD-nn` in the tasks of the work package it blocks, with the documented partial-proceed behaviour where one exists |

The per-phase-group convention blocks below add detail; where one of them names an abbreviation differently from this table, this table governs and the difference is a plan-review finding.

---

## Phase P0 Foundation and Phase P1 Containerized infrastructure

**Source**: docs/21 §3.3 (P0, P1), §4, §5.1, §5.2, §8, §10; detailed steps in docs/02, 04, 06, 11, 12, 15, 16, 19, 20.

**Phase-group conventions (P0-P1)**

- `$FEAT` = `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation`; `$EV` = `$FEAT/evidence` (ledger, anchors, blobs, verdicts, docs/06 §11); `$AUD` = `$FEAT/audit`.
- `RUNP <IMG-ID> -- <cmd>` = `scripts/containers/run_pinned.sh <IMG-ID> -- <cmd>` (delivered by T007): resolves `<IMG-ID>` to `reference@sha256:<digest>` from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml`, runs rootless podman with `--userns=keep-id --cap-drop=ALL --memory --memory-swap --cpus --pids-limit --label project=catalogizer --label op_id=<id>`, `-v "$PWD":/src:ro -v <out>:/out:rw` (`<out>` from `--out <dir>`, default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`); refuses a tag-only or unresolved image; `--rw docs` adds `-v "$PWD/docs":/src/docs:rw`, the only writable source bind it accepts, for the register writes of `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064).
- Every executable artifact is test-first (§11.4.224): the test task precedes the implementation task and the RED run is captured before code exists; every gate ships a paired §1.1 mutation that is observed failing before the gate is trusted (G-GATE).
- Until `tools/evidence/evrec` exists (WP-05), evidence is a transcript under `$EV/<wp>/` plus `$EV/<wp>/SHA256SUMS`; T095 (P0 exit gate) re-records every P0 transcript as an `ev/1` ledger entry (`$FEAT/contracts/evidence-record.schema.json`).
- Image-to-test binding: IMG-TESTUTIL (bash, git, sqlite3, python3 with `jsonschema`, `PyYAML` and `pytest`, jq) runs every P0 test that needs those tools; IMG-KCOV is kcov built on the IMG-TESTUTIL base (T006), so a coverage run also has git, jq and python3; IMG-SHELLCHECK only lints. T008 checks every (test, image) pair, not a union over images.
- Commit path: before CPA adoption is recorded (T047, after the T046 G-GATE GO), commits are plain fast-forward commits on `main` (never `--force`, never a rewrite); from T047 on every commit goes through `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (docs/21 IC-16) in commit windows (below). No commit lands on `main` before HC-0 (T014): docs/21 §3.3 makes plan acceptance at HC-0 the P0 entry; this list lets the read-only, preparation and test-first tasks start before T014 so that the owner request list (T013) exists to present at HC-0 (a recorded deviation), and every committing step of P0 depends on T014.
- Read-only `git`, CodeGraph CLI reads, Lumen read-only calls, the read-only verifier and the control-plane `podman run` launcher run on the host (docs/16 §5, §18); everything that compiles, tests, lints, scans or renders runs in a pinned rootless container.
- `[REVIEW]` tasks are owned by stream ST-REV (Opus, xhigh, §11.4.209), never by the producing stream, iterated to zero blocking findings (§11.4.134), verdict files under `$EV/reviews/` in the review-verdict format of the abbreviation table (`covers_runs` included, empty for a review that covers no held commit).
- A path a P0-P1 task creates that does not exist at the plan commit is new and a deliverable of the task that names it, whether or not the text marks it `(new)` (same convention as the P4-P5 block).
- `BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn` means the step waits for the owner (docs/21 §8); a documented research.md default is named where one exists; a missing device, host, credential or service is recorded `blocked-unavailable` with its reason, never a pass (FR-025). `BLOCKED-ON OD-nn` does the same for the 15 open ungrouped decisions of docs/21 §8.6.
- ODG-34 (quota and budget) applies from P0 onward as a reversible working default under the docs/21 planning-rule-6 exception (docs/21 §8, "ODG-34 and ODG-35 in use"): at most 6 working agents, Sonnet as the default working model, Opus at xhigh for every review, the doc02 §13 budget rules. No `research.md` default backs it; it stays an `Operator-blocked` item the owner may overturn, and overturning it changes scheduling only, never an evidence rule.
- Disk headroom (§12.9; measured at plan review: root filesystem 96% used with 86 GB free, podman storage 76 GB): every `podman build`, `podman pull` and container run (RUNP, and the tasks that build or pull images: T006, T106-T113, T114, T143, T144, T149, T151, T211, T270) first runs `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh --op <op_id>` (T001), which reads the free bytes of the podman graphroot (`podman info --format '{{.Store.GraphRoot}}'`) and of the repository filesystem, refuses below the absolute byte threshold `min_free_bytes` in `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.conf` (never a percentage; derived from measured image sizes, T001) with reason `disk_below_headroom`, and records the before and after values in `$EV/disk/<op_id>.json`; every image this list builds carries `--label project=catalogizer`, and cleanup removes only images and containers with that label (`podman image prune --filter label=project=catalogizer`), never a global prune (§11.4.122).
- Tracked deviations until the owner answers (registered by T092, listed in the P0 and P1 exit records, never reported compliant): (a) §11.4.173 places image production on the remote build host; until ODG-07 is answered (its options include 'local containers only', presented in T013), images are built and containers run on the local host in rootless podman; (b) §11.4.76 and §11.4.161 make the containers submodule the sole orchestration layer; RUNP and the runners compose `podman run` directly unless T002 finds a run primitive in `submodules/containers` to wrap, and the missing primitive is proposed upstream with T139 (§11.4.74).
- Single writer (docs/04 §12.2): from T069 on, every mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command against `$DB` runs through `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064), which runs it in IMG-TESTUTIL through `RUNP --rw docs`; read-only queries use the `immutable=1` URI (T071) only right after a checkpoint with no writer active, because that URI does not read rows still in the `-wal` file (docs/04 §12.2; reproduced in the round-10 review: 2 rows through `immutable=1` where `mode=ro` gave 3) and `mode=ro` cannot run on the read-only `/src` mount, where it would create `-shm` and `-wal` files: a read is either one `locked.sh` call that runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` and then the query, or a reader that refuses (20) while `docs/workable_items.db-wal` is non-empty.
- Repository-wide checks that fail on the current tree (the anti-bluff scan, revision headers, `check_pins.sh`, the QA bank validator) run as ratchets against their measured baselines under the bootstrap rule of the abbreviation table, never as whole-repository pass/fail stages that would refuse every commit; fixtures that violate such a check on purpose follow the deliberate-violation fixture convention of the same table (T040a). Whether one of these checks, or one of the plain checks taken from pre-commit, applies to a file at all is decided by its check class (abbreviation table, rule (V), T040b), never by an exemption written into a task. A plain check is not a ratchet: an existing file that already fails one when a task first declares it (measured in the round-10 review: `README.md` and `build-scripts/build-all.sh` lack a final newline, and `docker-compose.security.yml` has two trailing-whitespace lines and no final newline) is fixed by that task in the same change set and the fix named in its evidence (T036, T123, T148). The revision-header check is a ratchet that applies to every declared Markdown file whatever its row (T040; plan owner's decision after the round-11 reviews: a header is added when a file is next edited): a task that edits an existing tracked Markdown file without the §11.4.44 header adds it in the same change set, which lowers the file's baseline row (measured for rev 12: `README.md`, edited by T036, `.specify/memory/constitution.md`, edited by T081, and `docs/BUILD_CONTAINER_AUTO_DISPATCH.md`, edited by T122, carry none), except a file of class `generated` or `legacy-collection` (check class row).
- Every task that writes a `docs/scripts/*.md` companion adds its row to the index `docs/scripts/README.md` in the same change; T036 creates the index and lists the companions that exist when it runs.
- Commit windows (from T047 on; §11.4.84): the working tree is shared by every stream, and CPA refuses an uncommitted file that its declared change set does not name (13 at S7, 20 at the S0 sweep once T093 wires it), so commits are batched: at a window every stream stops writing, releases its long-op registrations and hands the conductor (ST-GOV) all of its uncommitted paths, each with the review verdict it still waits for, if any; the conductor runs CPA with their union as `--paths-from` (the stream of each path named in the commit message): the paths with no pending review form the first commit and are pushed, the paths still waiting for a review are committed after them as held commits (`Awaits-Review:`, T042) whose push waits until that verdict is committed in the main-repository HEAD with `verdict: GO` and the held commit's run in its `covers_runs` (review verdict row); work inside a submodule is held only through a `CPA --repo <path>` run, its pointer moving later through G-PIN (T042, T042a); after every window the tracked tree is clean and no unreviewed change has reached a remote; a held commit also holds back the push of every later commit until its GO (S6, T042), so reviews of held work are scheduled first; a task that commits only after its review GO (for example T029) keeps that guarantee for the remotes: at an earlier window its in-flight paths become commits held on that review, and its commit step adds the rest together with the verdict; a clean-tree gate (T161, T225) runs right after a window. A review that ends in a NO-GO that abandons a held change: at the next window the producing stream runs `git revert --no-commit <held commits>`, `git revert --quit` and `git restore --staged -- <reverted paths>` (only the reverted content stays, unstaged, in the working tree; a plain `git revert` would make a trailer-less local commit that CPA refuses at S1 with 20, `unrecorded_local_commit`), and the conductor commits the reverted paths through CPA, declared and held on the same verdict file (never a history rewrite, §11.4.113; this procedure governs the P4-P5 bullet of the same rule and docs/16 §12.2.4); ST-REV then writes a GO into that verdict file whose `covers_runs` names the held commits and the revert commit and states that the net change pushed is none; that GO releases both, while a GO that names the held commits without the revert is refused at S5 before it is committed (20, `verdict_covers_incomplete`, review verdict row), so the abandoned change never reaches a remote without its revert and a NO-GO never blocks the pushes of `main` for good (T039 fixtures; this held form is the one NO-GO form of this plan). When a remote of the main repository moved while a held commit waits, CPA integrates the moved tip by a merge commit of its own, held on its own merge-review file `$EV/reviews/CPA-merge-<run_id>.json`, never by a rebase (T040, T042), and until that file releases it S6 pushes nothing below the merge to any remote (T041); in P0 to P2, ST-REV reviews every such file before the next window (Opus at xhigh: the merge brings in only the remote's commits and keeps every held change, and a resolved conflict is reviewed under §11.4.211) and writes GO with the merge run in its `covers_runs`, which the conductor declares in the next window's change set beside the held commits' own verdicts; in P3, T307 does it with G-AUDIT; so a foreign push that changes no path of the window's change set never stops the windows. One that does change such a path while the path holds an uncommitted change gives 12 (`ff_blocked_by_local_changes`) on every run until it is integrated, `--local-only` included, because S1 always runs; the remediation is to re-run the window with `--commit-before-integrate` (T042; plan owner's decision after the round-11 reviews): under the lock and after the §9.2 backup, CPA commits the declared change set as ordinary CPA commits, held where declared, and then runs the merge path, so the overlap becomes a merge, clean or a `merge_conflict` that a `--resolve-merge` run resolves; a hand commit (20, `unrecorded_local_commit`), a stash (13) or parking the edit and restoring it over the remote's change is never the remediation. Chained and binary stores, `$EV/ledger.jsonl`, `$EV/flake_ledger.jsonl`, `$EV/anchors.jsonl` and `$EV/deferrals.jsonl` (hash-chained or append-only lines) and `docs/workable_items.db` with its dump and exports, have a single writer: the stream that owns the store, writing it only in the conductor's checkout through the store's own tool (ST-QA through `tools/evidence/evrec`, the deferral tools of WP-05 and `scripts/qa/stress_new_tests.sh` of T563; ST-REG through `scripts/register/locked.sh` of WP-06), so a clone of another actor never writes them; a merge conflict in one of them is never resolved textually: because it means that a second writer broke this rule, its remediation starts with a question to the plan owner (§11.4.66), and the owning stream then re-records its local side on top of the remote side through that tool, which `integrate_merge.sh` checks before it commits the merge (20, `store_not_rerecorded`, T040).
- Container outputs: a containerized step writes only to `/out` (RUNP `--out <dir>`, default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`, T003); a host step copies each file the task keeps to its tracked path (under `$EV`, `$AUD` or the source tree) and records the source and destination sha256 in its `ev/1` entry, or in its `SHA256SUMS` line before the recorder exists; no container writes `/src`, except `scripts/register/locked.sh` through `RUNP --rw docs` (T064).
- New and edited Markdown files: every tracked `*.md` file a task creates or edits (evidence notes and `docs/scripts/*.md` companions included) carries the §11.4.44 revision header table (rows `Revision`, `Created`, `Last modified`, `Status`) in its first 40 lines, because the S3 revision-header check (T040) applies to every declared Markdown file, so it meets no new key and an edited legacy file gains its header in the change set that lowers its row; a file that a generator writes in the bold-line form `**Revision:**` and `**Last modified:**` (the register engine's export, T067) passes as well, and a file of a class to which the revision-header check does not apply (class `generated`, for example the engine's exports, and class `legacy-collection`, the `docs/issues` tickets and the S-12 root reports; check class row, T040b) is exempt through its class, never through a list kept by a task.

---

### WP-09 Bootstrap pinned-image slice for the P0 tests (stream ST-INFRA, size M, FR-021, SC-012)

No inputs (local rootless podman only; no remote host, no owner decision). Starts together with WP-02 and WP-03; WP-04, WP-05, WP-06 and every P0 test leg depend on it (docs/21 IC-35).

- [ ] T001 [P] [US3] [TDD] Write the failing test `scripts/containers/tests/test_probe_host.sh` then implement `scripts/containers/probe_host.sh` (local part of docs/16 §8.5: `/dev/kvm`, SELinux, `ulimit -u`, cgroup version, rootless flag, `user.slice` `memory.max`, `nproc`, `MemTotal`/`MemAvailable`) writing `$EV/host-probe.json`; in the same task, test-first `scripts/containers/tests/test_disk_headroom.sh` then `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh` and its threshold file `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.conf` (the §12.9 free-space minimum as consumer DATA; no numeric value for it was found in `Constitution.md` at plan time): the threshold is an absolute byte count `min_free_bytes`, never a percentage (measured at plan review: root filesystem 96% used with about 85 GB free, so any percentage floor above 4% would refuse every build), derived as twice the largest measured size of an image this list uses plus the largest measured build scratch peak, with the formula, its inputs and their measurement dates written in the file (first derived here from `podman image inspect --format '{{.Size}}'` of the images already in local storage or, when none is present, from the layer sizes the registry manifests of the four WP-09 base images report, read without pulling, the factor two being the stated margin for their compression; re-derived by T006 once the WP-09 images exist and by T115 once `build/containers/profile.json` holds the measured peaks), reviewed in T010; a caller also passes `--need <bytes>`, the size of the image it pulls or builds (from the lock entry, or from the registry manifest before a first pull); with `podman info` and `df` shims a graphroot or repository filesystem whose free bytes minus the need fall below the threshold is refused with reason `disk_below_headroom` and a non-zero exit, a value above it passes, a percentage value in the conf file is refused as a configuration error, before and after values go to `$EV/disk/<op_id>.json` or, when `DISK_HEADROOM_OUT_DIR` is set, to that directory (CPA sets it to `$CPA_RUN/disk/`, T042), and an unreadable free-space value is refused, never treated as enough; RED: the test run before `probe_host.sh` exists exits non-zero and is captured to `$EV/wp09/probe-red.txt`; a control needle reads a path that must not exist and records the expected absence; an errored probe is `error`, never `absent`; a host without SELinux (no `/sys/fs/selinux`, no `getenforce`) is recorded `absent`, never `error`; `mkdir -p $EV/wp09` precedes the RED run (host shell is allowed here, docs/21 §3.3 P0)

## Phase P2: Inventory and baselines (doc21 §3.3 P2, §5.3)

**Entry**: WP-06 register operational, WP-05 recorder available, WP-13 stack available for baseline test runs.
**Exit (doc21 §3.3)**: `SELECT count(*) FROM v_unmapped_entries` returns 0 and the doc04 §13.1 per-source totals show `entries = mapped` for every `reg_sources` row (docs/21 IC-39: the docs/03 staging query on `src_entry ... disposition='PENDING'` names a table the docs/04 v4 DDL does not have; there the entries live in `reg_source_entries` and the 'no PENDING entry' rule is this view); every source row mapped with its `reg_source_map.relation` in the closed set; every docs/21 §9.1 seed and every §9.5 entry a mapped source entry; every external tracker recorded `queried` or `skipped(reason)`; `applicability.yaml` with no `?` cell; coverage baseline per application recorded. **Human checkpoint HC-2** (T222, `$EV/hc/HC-2.json`): SC-001 reconciliation reviewed; ODG-09 legacy-closure policy and doc04 D-6 category mapping confirmed; the reviewed exit record `$EV/p2-exit.json` (T220, T221) precedes it.

**Phase-group conventions (P2-P3)** (abbreviations only, no new mechanism):
- `$EV` = `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation/evidence/` (doc06 §11 layout); every PASS/FAIL below is an `ev/1` entry in `$EV/ledger.jsonl` written by the recorder `tools/evidence/evrec` (WP-05), validated against `$FEAT/contracts/evidence-record.schema.json`.
- `$WI` = `submodules/constitution/scripts/workable-items/bin/workable-items`; `$DB` = `docs/workable_items.db` (ODG-11 default, OD-11). Items are created only through the pre-minted `reg_ids` path of doc04 §12.3 step 1, and every mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command runs through `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064), including the minting of `FND-NNNN` ids by the P3 agents, of which up to six run at once.
- "runner(IMG-x)" = the WP-12 container runner wrapper for image IMG-x (rootless podman, digest from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml`, `--memory` and `--pids-limit` set, `jobs=1` until per-job memory is measured, doc16 §8.2). Its exact script path is fixed by WP-12: `scripts/containers/run_<toolchain>.sh` (T120) and the lane dispatcher `scripts/test-in-container.sh <app> <lane> -- <cmd>` (TIC, T121); no task here invents another. Git, index reads and the read-only verifier run on the host (doc16 §18); nothing builds or tests on the bare host (§11.4.173).
- Gate scripts: `scripts/register/gate.sh` (doc04 §12.3 step 2; `--completion` mode for step 3, T180); exports through `scripts/register/export.sh` and `reconcile.sh` (T067). The disk-headroom precondition, the tracked deviations, the ratchet rule, the commit windows, the container-output rule and the new-Markdown header rule of the P0-P1 conventions apply here unchanged. Commits only through `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (WP-04, IC-16) in commit windows, on `main`, fast-forward to every upstream, never force; with up to six P3 agents writing at once, each stream's uncommitted files wait for the next window, and a clean-tree gate (T161, T225) runs right after one.
- File names marked "(new)" are proposed here where the source document names the tool but not its path; the reviewer of the WP confirms the path. Every other path a P2-P3 task creates that does not exist at the plan commit is likewise new and a deliverable of the task that names it, mark or no mark.
- `[REVIEW]` tasks are owned by stream ST-REV on Opus at xhigh (§11.4.209), never by the producing stream (§11.4.240); every verdict file follows the review-verdict row of the abbreviation table (`covers_runs` included).
- Audit work reaches no remote before G-AUDIT (rev 9): from T225 until the WP-39R GO verdicts are committed (T307), every path of WP-30 to WP-39 that a commit window takes is a held commit: audit records (`$AUD/`, `$EV/` and the register writes of P3) on `$EV/reviews/WP-39R.json` and code, tests and configuration on `$EV/reviews/WP-39R-code.json` (T306), except the WP-37 tooling that T287 has reviewed (T288 commits it after that GO); a held commit holds back the push of every later commit (S6, T042), so pushes of `main` wait for G-AUDIT, which is scheduled right after WP-39; when another clone pushes `main` in that period, the next window's CPA run integrates the moved tip by a merge commit held on its own merge-review file `$EV/reviews/CPA-merge-<run_id>.json` (rule (Y), T042), which does not stop the windows while the push changes no path of the window's change set, and a push that does is integrated by re-running the window with `--commit-before-integrate` (P0-P1 conventions, T042), and ST-REV reviews those merge files together with G-AUDIT, so the T307 push needs their GO verdicts as well.

## Phase P3: Audit pass (doc21 §3.3 P3, §5.4)

**Entry**: P1 detectors pinned with needles (WP-15); index gate PASS in the current pass; register accepting findings. **Parallelism**: up to six read-only audit agents, one per unit (doc02 §14.3 waves W1-W3); heavy detectors queued at K = min(floor(18 GiB / measured peak RSS), 4); one Android container at a time; `ulimit -u` headroom checked before each wave (§12.12). **Exit**: every unit has a recorded audit result; second run from an identical manifest yields `IDENTICAL` (SC-002); every finding carries location, severity, category, evidence and register link (FR-007). **HC-3**: owner accepts the audit report. Every detector has golden-good, golden-bad and a control needle before any zero result is trusted (doc06 §10, §11.4.201(7)(b)). Each finding is a `finding/1` record (`$FEAT/contracts/finding.schema.json`) with an `FND-NNNN` id minted from `reg_ids` through `scripts/register/locked.sh`, written once as `$AUD/findings/<FND-NNNN>.json` (the finding store of doc02 §9 and data-model); a later run that finds the same `fingerprint` appends its run id to that file's `run_ids` and mints nothing (§11.4.214). The per-run `$AUD/runs/<run-id>/findings.index.jsonl` is a derived projection (doc02 §9): one line per finding with `unit`, `path`, `line_start`, `rule_id` and `fingerprint`, sorted by `(unit, path, line_start, rule_id)`, with no timestamps, no `run_ids` and no minted id, so two runs on identical input give byte-identical index files (SC-002). The disk-headroom precondition and the single-writer rule of the P0-P1 conventions apply to every P3 container run and register write.

**Audited input of the two audit runs (SC-002; decided in rev 9, replacing the UNCONFIRMED note that rev 8 placed in T300; docs/21 IC-47 withdrew the docs/02 §12.1 rev 5 exclusion list and left the question open).** `AUD-001` and `AUD-002` audit one input, the input commit that T225 records (the main-repository HEAD right after the commit window that opens P3) together with the submodule commits its gitlinks record, identified by `input_tree`: per repository, the sha256 of the sorted `git ls-tree -r <commit>` lines of the paths that exist at the input commit and lie outside the excluded paths of `$AUD/input-exclusions.txt`, computed by `scripts/audit/input_tree.sh` (T225) for the input commit and, at the start of `AUD-002`, for the current HEAD. The exclusion list is written in T225 before `AUD-001` starts, is not changed until `AUD-002` ends (its sha256 is in both manifests) and is reviewed in T303; it names the audit's own outputs (`$AUD/`, `$EV/`, `docs/workable_items.db`, `docs/register/`), the plan set `$FEAT/`, and the existing files that P3 tasks are allowed to change: `scripts/repo/validate_checks.tsv` (T283a), `scripts/repo/fixture_roots.txt` (T235), `docs/scripts/README.md` (T248a, T283a), and `docs/DOC_SCOPE.yaml`, `docs/reference/evidence/` and `docs/security/rotation-ledger.md` where they exist at the input commit (T265, T281, T282, T286). A file that a P3 task creates is not part of the input, because only paths of the input commit count. `main_head` is recorded in both manifests but not compared, so a commit window during P3 leaves the audited input unchanged as long as it changes no input path. Every detector takes its unit's file set from `git ls-tree -r --name-only <input commit>` limited to the unit's paths and the input, never from a working-tree glob, so a file a P3 task creates belongs to no unit (T306 reviews it); the link crawler treats a link into an excluded path as external and does not read its target. An audit step that has to change an input file, or add a file inside an application or module tree that a detector builds or tests as a whole directory (`catalog-api`, `catalog-web`, the Tauri and Android trees, the submodules), never does so in the working tree during P3: it applies the change as a patch `$AUD/patches/<task>.patch` to a scratch copy of the unit (the doc08 §6.1 writable-copy pattern), `AUD-002` applies the same patch set (each patch's sha256 in both manifests; class `patches` of rule (V), T040b: a patch is committed byte-exact, its blank context lines being single spaces, so the whitespace and end-of-file checks do not apply to it), and the change reaches the source tree only after `AUD-002`, through T302 (held on the T306 code review); this binds T229, T238, T249, T292 and T293 and any other P3 task in the same position. Every unit result records the image digests and the sha256 of each tooling file its detectors ran (`tooling`), and a unit whose listed tooling changed after its `AUD-001` result is re-run under `AUD-001` before T300, so both runs of every unit use the same tooling. T299 checks that every path changed since the input commit (`git diff --name-only <input commit> HEAD` and the uncommitted files) is an excluded path or a path added after the input commit outside every application and module tree; T300 refuses to start while `input_tree` differs. Because the detectors read checkouts while `input_tree` hashes committed trees, both runs also fix the checkouts (rev 10, round-9 review): T225 and T300 require, in the clean-tree measurement of every input repository, no pointer drift and no row of `.audit/pending_pins.tsv` (`summary.pin_drift` 0, so each checkout's HEAD equals the gitlink the input commit records), and each manifest records, as an identity field, the sha256 of every file of an excepted dirty row of `scripts/repo/exceptions.tsv` (the docling case), so a checkout that differs from the hashed trees, or excepted dirt that changes between the runs, makes the manifests differ. A commit window during P3 that has to integrate a remote move does so by a CPA merge commit (P2-P3 conventions); its incoming changes reach the audited input only when they change an input path, which T299 and the `input_tree` comparison then report. This design is presented to the plan owner with the plan at HC-0 (T013, T014; docs/21 IC-47 leaves the state vector to the plan owner), and docs/02 §12.1, §12.2 and §20 (rev 7) and docs/21 IC-47, §12.2 and §12.10 (rev 11) are reconciled to this block.
